async function nativeHost(verb: "dev" | "serve", prefix: string[], options: ProofOptions): Promise<ProofHost & { personas?: string[] }> {
  const env = { ...process.env, ...options.env }, id = randomUUID();
  const ports = portRange(env), timeout = deadline(options.timeoutMs, 30_000);
  const args = [...(options.args ?? [])];
  if (!args.includes("--port")) args.push("--port", ports);
  const run = logged(resolveBinary(env), [verb, ...prefix, ...args], { ...options, processGroup: true, env: { ...env, SNAPBACK4_PROOF_ID: id, SNAPBACK4_SUPERVISOR_PID: String(process.pid) } });
  try {
    await bounded(run.started, timeout, "proof process-group handshake");
    const url = await ready(run, line => {
      const match = line.match(/^SNAPBACK4_READY verb=(dev|serve) generation=\d+ url=(http:\/\/[^ ]+) pid=(\d+)(?: |$)/);
      if (!match) return;
      if (match[1] !== verb || Number(match[3]) !== run.pid()) throw Error("proof ready identity mismatch");
      return match[2];
    }, timeout);
    const { response, body } = await fetchIdentity(`${url}/auth/config`, timeout);
    if (!response.ok || body.proof_id !== id) throw Error("proof served identity mismatch");
    if (verb === "dev" && body.devDoors === false) process.stderr.write("snapback4: password-only development doors CLOSED (/seed, /dev/*, personas). For shared previews and probe:serve run snapback4 deploy staging --staging, then startServe(\"staging\"); see snapback4 guide staging. Local fixtures require dev --unsafe-dev-doors.\n");
    let personas: string[] = [];
    if (verb === "dev" && body.devDoors !== false) {
      const status = await fetchIdentity(`${url}/dev/status`, timeout);
      if (status.body.pid !== run.pid()) throw Error("proof dev PID mismatch");
      if (!Array.isArray(status.body.personas) || !status.body.personas.every(name => typeof name === "string")) throw Error("proof dev personas missing");
      personas = status.body.personas;
    }
    return { url, ...(verb === "dev" ? { personas } : {}), pid: run.pid()!, logs: run.logs, stop: () => stopChild(run.child, deadline(options.stopTimeoutMs, 5000)) };
  } catch (error) { await stopChild(run.child, deadline(options.stopTimeoutMs, 5000)); throw error; }
}
export interface DevHost extends ProofHost {
  personas: string[];
  /** Read the current authoritative development time, including all clock advances. */
  now(): Promise<number>;
  advance(duration: string, options?: AdvanceOptions): Promise<ClockAdvance>;
  drain(options?: Pick<AdvanceOptions, "eachBoundary" | "allowHeld">): Promise<ClockAdvance>;
  operatorToken(service?: string, days?: number): Promise<string>;
}
/** The array form preserves the former startDev(args) recipe. */
/** @throws invalid port range or conflicting memory flags; child exits, readiness deadline, PID/served identity mismatch, or unavailable dev clock. */
export function startDev(options: ProofOptions | readonly string[] = {}): Promise<DevHost> {
  const opts: ProofOptions = Array.isArray(options) ? { args: options } : options as ProofOptions;
  const args = [...(opts.args ?? [])];
  const persistent = args.some(arg => arg === "--store" || arg.startsWith("--store=") || arg === "--no-memory");
  if (persistent && args.includes("--memory")) throw new TypeError("proof startDev: --memory conflicts with --store/--no-memory");
  // Native persistence is the default. --no-memory remains a consumed explicit alias.
  const host = nativeHost("dev", [], { ...opts, args: args.filter(arg => arg !== "--no-memory") });
  return host.then(host => {
    const operatorToken = async (service = "operator", days = 1) => {
      if (!/^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,64}$/.test(service) || !Number.isSafeInteger(days) || days < 1 || days > 3650) throw Error("proof operatorToken: invalid service or days (1..3650)");
      const port = new URL(host.url).port;
      const result = await cli(["token", "--service", service, "--days", String(days), "--port", port, "--quiet"], opts);
      if (result.code !== 0) throw Error(`proof development operator token refused: ${result.stderr}`);
      const token = result.stdout.trim();
      if (!/^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{43}$/.test(token)) throw Error("proof development operator token malformed");
      return token;
    };
    const clockCall = async (body?: object): Promise<ClockAdvance> => {
      const response = await fetch(`${host.url}/dev/clock/${body ? "advance" : "status"}`, { method: body ? "POST" : "GET", headers: { authorization: `Bearer ${await operatorToken()}`, "content-type": "application/json" }, ...(body ? { body: JSON.stringify(body) } : {}), signal: AbortSignal.timeout(deadline(opts.timeoutMs, 60_000)) });
      const result = await response.json() as Partial<ClockAdvance> & {denied?:{code?:string;message?:string}};
      if (!response.ok || result.denied || !Number.isSafeInteger(result.now)) throw Object.assign(Error(`development clock refused or reply unavailable; inspect before retrying: ${result.denied?.code ?? response.status} ${result.denied?.message ?? ""}`), {report:result});
      const now = result.now!;
      return { now, fired: result.fired ?? [], held: result.held ?? [], maintenancePaused: result.maintenancePaused ?? false, valueOf() { return now; } };
    };
    return { ...host, personas: host.personas!, operatorToken, now: async () => (await clockCall()).now,
      advance: (duration: string, advanceOptions: AdvanceOptions = {}) => {
        if (typeof duration !== "string" || (!/^\+?\d+(ms|s|m|h|d|w)$/.test(duration) || BigInt(duration.replace(/\D/g, "")) === 0n)) throw new TypeError("E_PROOF_USAGE: advance requires a positive whole duration");
        return clockCall({duration, ...advanceOptions});
      },
      drain: (advanceOptions: Pick<AdvanceOptions,"eachBoundary"|"allowHeld"> = {}) => clockCall({duration:"0ms",drain:true,...advanceOptions}),
    };
  });
}
export interface ScheduleOutcome {
  name: string;
  target: string;
  period: string;
  fired: boolean;
  reason: string;
  holdCode?: "disabled" | "saved-boundary" | "initialized" | "not-due" | "maintenance-failed";
  savedBoundary?: number;
  nextBoundary: string;
  heldUntil?: string;
  periodStart?: number;
  periodEnd?: number;
  firedWindows: { periodStart: number; periodEnd: number }[];
  skippedPeriods?: number;
  skippedBoundaries: { periodStart: number; periodEnd: number; command: string }[];
  skippedBoundariesOmitted?: number;
  run?: { id: string; state: string; rowsWritten: number; error: string | null; errorTruncated: boolean };
}
export interface ClockAdvance { now: number; fired: ScheduleOutcome[]; held: ScheduleOutcome[]; maintenancePaused: boolean; valueOf(): number }
export interface AdvanceOptions { eachBoundary?: boolean; drain?: boolean; allowHeld?: boolean; sessions?: "preserve" | "expire" }
export interface ServeHost extends ProofHost {
  readonly deploymentDir: string;
  /** Authoritative staging time; requires a staging store and local operator authority. */
  now(): Promise<number>;
  /** Advance staging time once and drain due work. A lost reply must not be retried. */
  advance(duration: string, options?: AdvanceOptions): Promise<ClockAdvance>;
  /** Resume and drain maintenance without advancing time. */
  drain(options?: Pick<AdvanceOptions, "eachBoundary" | "allowHeld">): Promise<ClockAdvance>;
  operatorToken(service?: string, days?: number): Promise<string>;
  seedAccounts(options: SeedAccountsOptions): Promise<StagingSessions>;
  provisionAccounts(options: ProvisionAccountsOptions): Promise<ProvisionedSessions>;
  sessions?: StagingSessions;
}
export interface ServeOptions extends ProofOptions { seedUsers?: SeedAccountsOptions; provisionUsers?: ProvisionAccountsOptions; clockOffset?: string }
// @ref LLP 3000.000.000.001#2-real-staging-accounts — operator authority stays in the existing local store proof; bulk setup refuses production.
/** Starts a release. stop() awaits native exit and kernel store release.
 * @throws E_PROOF_USAGE for missing deployment directory; child exit, readiness deadline or served identity mismatch. stop() rejects bounded cleanup failure. */
export async function startServe(deployDir: string, options: ServeOptions | readonly string[] = {}): Promise<ServeHost> {
  if (typeof deployDir !== "string" || !deployDir.trim()) throw Object.assign(new TypeError("E_PROOF_USAGE: startServe(deployDir, options) requires a deployment directory as its first argument"), { code: "E_PROOF_USAGE" });
  const opts: ServeOptions = Array.isArray(options) ? { args: options } : options as ServeOptions;
  const directory = resolve(opts.cwd ?? ".", deployDir);
  if (opts.clockOffset !== undefined && (typeof opts.clockOffset !== "string" || !/^[+-]?\d+(ms|s|m|h|d|w)$/.test(opts.clockOffset))) throw new TypeError("E_PROOF_USAGE: clockOffset is a signed duration such as +25h");
  if (opts.clockOffset !== undefined && opts.args?.some(arg => arg === "--clock-offset" || arg.startsWith("--clock-offset="))) throw new TypeError("E_PROOF_USAGE: pass clockOffset once");
  const host = await nativeHost("serve", [directory], { ...opts, args: [...(opts.args ?? []), ...(opts.clockOffset === undefined ? [] : ["--clock-offset", opts.clockOffset])] });
  const clockCall = async (body?: object): Promise<ClockAdvance> => {
    const config = await fetchIdentity(`${host.url}/auth/config`, deadline(opts.timeoutMs, 10_000));
    if (!config.response.ok || config.body.environment !== "staging") throw Error("E_CLOCK_DEV_ONLY: clock controls require a deploy --staging store");
    const clockToken = served.operatorToken("clock");
    const response = await fetch(`${host.url}/dev/clock/${body ? "advance" : "status"}`, { method: body ? "POST" : "GET", headers: { Authorization: `Bearer ${await clockToken}`, "Content-Type": "application/json" }, ...(body ? { body: JSON.stringify(body) } : {}), signal: AbortSignal.timeout(deadline(opts.timeoutMs, 60_000)) });
    const result = await response.json() as Partial<ClockAdvance> & { sessionNotice?: string; denied?: { code?: string; message?: string } };
    if (result.sessionNotice) process.stderr.write(`snapback4: ${result.sessionNotice}\n`);
    if (!response.ok || result.denied || !Number.isSafeInteger(result.now)) throw Object.assign(Error(`staging clock refused or reply unavailable; inspect clock and queued work before retrying: ${result.denied?.code ?? response.status} ${result.denied?.message ?? ""}`), { report: result });
    if (body && (!Array.isArray(result.fired) || !Array.isArray(result.held))) throw Error("staging clock reply has no schedule report; inspect clock and queued work before retrying");
    for (const schedule of result.held ?? []) if (schedule.holdCode === "saved-boundary") process.stderr.write(`snapback4: schedule ${schedule.name}: ${schedule.reason}\n`);
    const report: ClockAdvance = { now: result.now!, fired: result.fired ?? [], held: result.held ?? [], maintenancePaused: result.maintenancePaused ?? false, valueOf() { return this.now; } };
    return report;
  };
  // Exit can precede inherited watchdog flock release. The private local
  // barrier observes the same canonical ownership sidecar without writing data.
  let stopping: Promise<void> | undefined;
  const stop = () => stopping ??= (async () => {
    await host.stop();
    const result = await cli(["serve", directory, "--wait-stopped"], {...opts, timeoutMs:6000});
    if (result.code !== 0) throw Error(`proof serve store release failed: ${result.stderr}`);
  })().catch(error => { stopping = undefined; throw error; });
  const served: ServeHost = { ...host, stop, deploymentDir: directory,
    now: async () => (await clockCall()).now,
    advance: (duration, options = {}) => {
      if (typeof duration !== "string" || (!/^\+?\d+(ms|s|m|h|d|w)$/.test(duration) || BigInt(duration.replace(/\D/g, "")) === 0n)) throw new TypeError("E_PROOF_USAGE: advance requires a positive whole duration");
      return clockCall({ duration, ...options });
    },
    drain: (options = {}) => clockCall({ duration: "0ms", drain: true, ...options }),
    operatorToken: async (service = "operator", days = 1) => {
      if (!/^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,64}$/.test(service) || !Number.isSafeInteger(days) || days < 1 || days > 3650) throw Error("proof operatorToken: invalid service or days (1..3650)");
      const result = await cli(["token", "--service", service, "--days", String(days), "--release", directory, "--quiet"], opts);
      if (result.code !== 0) throw Error(`proof operator token refused: ${result.stderr}`);
      const token = result.stdout.trim();
      if (!/^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{43}$/.test(token)) throw Error("proof operator token malformed");
      return token;
    },
    seedAccounts: accountOptions => seedAccounts(served, accountOptions),
    provisionAccounts: accountOptions => provisionAccounts(served, accountOptions),
  };
  try {
    if (opts.seedUsers && opts.provisionUsers) throw new TypeError("E_PROOF_USAGE: choose seedUsers for staging or provisionUsers for production");
    if (opts.seedUsers) served.sessions = await served.seedAccounts(opts.seedUsers);
    if (opts.provisionUsers) served.sessions = await served.provisionAccounts(opts.provisionUsers);
    return served;
  }
  catch (error) { await served.stop(); throw error; }
}
async function frontend(kind: "static" | "vite", dir: string, options: ProofOptions): Promise<ProofHost> {
  const env = { ...process.env, ...options.env }, ports = portRange(env), timeout = deadline(options.timeoutMs, 30_000);
  const cwd = resolve(options.cwd ?? ".");
  const directory = resolve(cwd, dir);
  // Vite is resolved from the app, never the proof package's development dependencies.
  const vite = kind === "vite" ? createRequire(resolve(directory, "package.json")).resolve("vite") : "";
  const run = logged(process.execPath, [fileURLToPath(new URL("./proof-host.js", import.meta.url)), kind, directory, ports, vite], { ...options, env, detached: process.platform !== "win32", livenessPipe: true });
  try {
    const url = await ready(run, line => {
      const match = line.match(/^SNAPBACK4_PROOF_READY (\{.*\})$/);
      if (!match) return;
      const data = JSON.parse(match[1]!);
      if (data.pid !== run.child.pid || data.kind !== kind) throw Error("proof frontend PID mismatch");
      return data.url as string;
    }, timeout);
    const response = await fetch(url, { signal: AbortSignal.timeout(timeout) });
    if (response.headers.get("x-snapback4-proof") !== String(run.child.pid) + ":" + run.id) throw Error("proof frontend identity mismatch");
    await response.body?.cancel();
    return { url, pid: run.child.pid!, logs: run.logs, stop: () => stopChild(run.child, deadline(options.stopTimeoutMs, 5000)) };
  } catch (error) { await stopChild(run.child, deadline(options.stopTimeoutMs, 5000)); throw error; }
}
/** @throws missing export/index or build script precondition; exhausted ports, readiness deadline or served identity mismatch. */
export function startStatic(dir: string, options: ProofOptions = {}): Promise<ProofHost> { return frontend("static", dir, options); }
/** @throws missing Vite dependency/configuration; exhausted ports, readiness deadline or served identity mismatch. */
export function startVite(dir: string, options: ProofOptions = {}): Promise<ProofHost> { return frontend("vite", dir, options); }

export interface CliResult { code: number | null; signal: NodeJS.Signals | null; stdout: string; stderr: string; logs: ProofHost["logs"] }
/** Waits for close (stdio drained), never merges the two streams. Nonzero is returned for refusal assertions. */
/** @throws spawn error or command deadline; nonzero exit is returned in code, not thrown. */
export function cli(args: readonly string[], options: ProofOptions = {}): Promise<CliResult> {
  return command(process.execPath, [fileURLToPath(new URL("../bin/snapback4.js", import.meta.url)), ...args], { ...options, secret: options.secret || args[0] === "token" });
}
/** A bounded directly spawned build/tool command, with the same file logs. */
/** @throws spawn error or command deadline; nonzero exit is returned in code, not thrown. */
export async function command(binary: string, args: readonly string[], options: ProofOptions = {}): Promise<CliResult> {
  const run = logged(binary, args, { ...options, processGroup: true, env: { ...process.env, ...options.env } });
  try {
    await bounded(run.closed, deadline(options.timeoutMs, 120_000), "proof cli close");
    if (run.error()) throw run.error();
    return { code: run.code(), signal: run.signal(), stdout: run.stdout(), stderr: logText(run.logs.stderr), logs: run.logs };
  } catch (error) { await stopChild(run.child, deadline(options.stopTimeoutMs, 5000)); throw error; }
  finally { run.child.stdout?.destroy(); } // Secret capture must not pin the driver after a deadline.
}
async function successful(args: string[], options: ProofOptions) {
  const result = await cli(args, options);
  if (result.code !== 0) {
    const stdout = result.stdout.trimEnd();
    const tail = stdout.length > 4000 ? `…${stdout.slice(-4000)}` : stdout;
    throw Error(`proof CLI failed (${result.code}): ${result.stderr}; stdout tail: ${tail || "(empty)"}; logs: ${result.logs.stderr}`);
  }
  return result;
}
/** @throws missing explicit saved source, CLI failure, spawn error or deadline. */
export function deploy(dir: string, schema: string, options: ProofOptions = {}) {
  if (typeof schema !== "string" || !schema) throw Error(`proof deploy requires an explicit saved schema file or directory; got ${JSON.stringify(schema) ?? String(schema)}: did npm run day2:proof pass versions/v1?`);
  return successful(["deploy", dir, "--schema", schema, ...(options.args ?? [])], options);
}
/** @throws invalid explicit release target, CLI failure, spawn error or deadline. */
export function rollback(dir: string, to: number | "latest", options: ProofOptions = {}) {
  if (to !== "latest" && (!Number.isSafeInteger(to) || to < 1)) throw Error(`proof rollback requires an explicit immutable release target; got ${JSON.stringify(to) ?? String(to)}`);
  return successful(["rollback", dir, "--to", String(to), ...(options.args ?? [])], options);
}
// @ref LLP 3000.011.003.000.000#11-wave-14-truthful-starter-custody-day4 — saved-source proof results expose authored cases separately from query sweeps.
export interface TestSourceOptions extends ProofOptions { fixtureOnly?: boolean }
export interface TestSourceResult extends CliResult { casesRun: number; casesSkipped: number }
/** @throws missing source, CLI failure, invalid business-case summary or zero cases without fixtureOnly. */
export function testSource(source: string, options: TestSourceOptions = {}): Promise<TestSourceResult> {
  if (typeof source !== "string" || !source) throw Error(`proof testSource requires an explicit saved source file or directory; got ${JSON.stringify(source) ?? String(source)}: pass versions/v1`);
  const { fixtureOnly = false, ...proofOptions } = options;
  return successful(["test", "--source", source, ...(fixtureOnly ? ["--fixture-only"] : []), ...(proofOptions.args ?? [])], proofOptions).then(result => {
    const summaries = [...result.stdout.matchAll(/^SNAPBACK4_TEST_CASES (\{.*\})$/gm)];
    if (summaries.length !== 1) throw Error(`proof testSource received no unique business-case summary; stdout tail: ${result.stdout.trimEnd().slice(-4000) || "(empty)"}; logs: ${result.logs.stdout}`);
    let cases: { run: number; skipped: number };
    try { cases = JSON.parse(summaries[0]![1]!) as { run: number; skipped: number }; }
    catch { throw Error(`proof testSource received a malformed business-case summary: ${summaries[0]![0]}; logs: ${result.logs.stdout}`); }
    if (!Number.isSafeInteger(cases.run) || cases.run < 0 || !Number.isSafeInteger(cases.skipped) || cases.skipped < 0) {
      throw Error(`proof testSource received invalid business-case counts: ${summaries[0]![0]}; logs: ${result.logs.stdout}`);
    }
    if (cases.run === 0 && !fixtureOnly) {
      throw Error(`proof testSource ran no business cases (${cases.skipped} skipped); add test.cases.jsonl for this saved version, or pass { fixtureOnly: true } when fixture loading alone is the intended proof; stdout tail: ${result.stdout.trimEnd().slice(-4000) || "(empty)"}`);
    }
    return { ...result, casesRun: cases.run, casesSkipped: cases.skipped };
  });
}

/** @throws None; returns a boolean for recognized sandbox launch errors. */
export function sandboxUnavailable(error: unknown): boolean {
  return /No usable sandbox|Running as root without --no-sandbox|Failed to move to new namespace|SUID sandbox|sandbox.*Operation not permitted|Failed to unshare.*Operation not permitted/i.test(String(error));
}
/** @throws E_PROOF_BROWSER for missing playwright-core; invalid deadline, browser launch failure or callback error; bounded context/browser cleanup failure. */
export async function withBrowser<T>(options: LaunchOptions & Pick<ProofOptions, "cwd" | "logsDir"> & { chromium?: BrowserType }, proof: (context: BrowserContext) => Promise<T>): Promise<T> {
  const { chromium: suppliedChromium, executablePath, cwd: _cwd, logsDir: _logs, ...other } = options;
  const requireCaller = createRequire(resolve(options.cwd ?? process.cwd(), "package.json"));
  let caller = suppliedChromium;
  {
    for (const peer of ["playwright-core", "@playwright/test", "playwright"]) {
      let path: string;
      try { path = requireCaller.resolve(peer); }
      catch (error) { if ((error as NodeJS.ErrnoException).code === "MODULE_NOT_FOUND") continue; throw error; }
      const peerModule = await import(path);
      await registerRenderedSelector(peerModule.selectors ?? peerModule.default?.selectors);
      caller ??= (peerModule.chromium ?? peerModule.default?.chromium) as BrowserType;
      break;
    }
  }
  if (!caller) {
    try { caller = (await import("playwright-core")).chromium; }
    catch (cause) { throw new Error("E_PROOF_BROWSER: withBrowser requires playwright-core; run npm install --save-dev playwright-core before deploying or starting proof hosts", { cause }); }
  }
  const directory = logsDir(options); mkdirSync(directory, { recursive: true, mode: 0o700 });
  const err = openSync(join(directory, `${randomUUID()}.browser.stderr.log`), "wx", 0o600);
  const diagnostic = openSync(join(directory, `${randomUUID()}.witness.private.jsonl`), "wx", 0o600);

  const launch = { ...other, ...(executablePath === undefined ? {} : { executablePath }), timeout: deadline(options.timeout, 30_000), logger: {
    isEnabled: () => true,
    log: (name: string, severity: string, message: string | Error) => { writeSync(err, `${name} ${severity}: ${String(message)}\n`); },
  } };
  const args = [...(options.args ?? [])];
  if (process.getuid?.() === 0 && !args.includes("--no-sandbox")) args.push("--no-sandbox");
  // Playwright owns its browser child over a pipe, including parent-exit cleanup.
  // No WebSocket server or TCP debugging port is opened.
  let browser;
  try {
    try { browser = await caller.launch({ ...launch, args, chromiumSandbox: !args.includes("--no-sandbox") }); }
    catch (error) {
      if (args.includes("--no-sandbox") || !sandboxUnavailable(error)) throw error;
      browser = await caller.launch({ ...launch, args: [...args, "--no-sandbox"], chromiumSandbox: false });
    }
    let context: BrowserContext | undefined;
    try {
      context = await browser.newContext();
      await context.exposeBinding("__snapback4ProofDiagnostic", (_source, value: unknown) => {
        writeSync(diagnostic, `${JSON.stringify(value)}\n`);
      });
      // Init scripts run on each document, including HTTP redirect destinations,
      // before any application script samples its URL.
      await context.addInitScript(() => {
        if (window.top !== window || !/^https?:$/.test(location.protocol)) return;
        const url = new URL(location.href);
        url.searchParams.set("witness", "web");
        history.replaceState(history.state, "", url.href);
      });
      // The bridge function is self-contained after TypeScript erases types.
      // Proof code lives in this realm, outside the application's bundle.
      await context.addInitScript({ content: webWitnessObserverScript() });
      context.setDefaultTimeout(30_000); context.setDefaultNavigationTimeout(30_000);
      return await proof(context);
    } finally {
      try { if (context) await bounded(context.close(), 5000, "browser context close"); }
      finally { await bounded(browser.close(), 5000, "browser close"); }
    }
  } finally { closeSync(err); closeSync(diagnostic); }
}
/** Keep owned proof hosts alive until SIGTERM/SIGINT, then stop all of them.
 * @throws AggregateError if any bounded host cleanup fails. */
// @ref LLP 3000.011.003.000.000#15-wave-26-proof-ergonomics-dx9 — a pending promise alone cannot keep probe:serve alive.
export async function serveUntilSignal(hosts: readonly Pick<ProofHost, "stop">[]): Promise<void> {
  if (!Array.isArray(hosts) || hosts.length === 0 || hosts.some(host => typeof host?.stop !== "function")) throw Error("E_PROOF_USAGE: serveUntilSignal requires at least one owned host");
  let finish!: () => void;
  const signalled = new Promise<void>(resolve => { finish = resolve; });
  const hold = setInterval(() => {}, 60_000);
  process.on("SIGTERM", finish); process.on("SIGINT", finish);
  try { await signalled; }
  finally {
    clearInterval(hold);
    try { await cleanup(...[...hosts].reverse().map(host => () => host.stop())); }
    finally { process.removeListener("SIGTERM", finish); process.removeListener("SIGINT", finish); }
  }
}
const substringLocators = new WeakMap<Locator, { locator: Locator; scoped: boolean }>();
/** Match the smallest visible element whose composed descendant text matches.
 * Strings normalize whitespace and match substrings by default; regexes keep their
 * own semantics. Editable values never count. Independent matches remain
 * ambiguous, including repeated rows. */
/** @throws None at construction; locator observation can refuse ambiguous or invalid selectors. */
// @ref LLP 3000.011.003.000.000#15-wave-26-proof-ergonomics-dx9 — substrings retain strict ambiguity and explicit exactness.
export function renderedText(page: Page, text: string | RegExp, options: { within?: Locator; exact?: boolean } | null = {}): Locator {
  const normalized = options ?? {};
  const root = normalized.within ?? page;
  const match = (exact: boolean) => registeredLocator(root.locator(`${renderedSelector}=${JSON.stringify({
    source: typeof text === "string" ? text : text.source,
    flags: typeof text === "string" ? "" : text.flags,
    regex: typeof text !== "string", exact,
  })}`));
  const locator = match(normalized.exact ?? false);
  if (normalized.within) scopedLocators.add(locator);
  if (typeof text === "string" && normalized.exact === true) substringLocators.set(locator, { locator: match(false), scoped: normalized.within !== undefined });
  return locator;
}

export interface ExhaustPagesOptions {
  /** Refuse rather than loop forever when a backend returns bad continuations. */
  maxPages?: number;
  /** Names the read in a refusal without exposing its rows or cursor. */
  label?: string;
}
type ProofPage<Row> =
  | { readonly data: readonly Row[]; readonly next?: Cursor | null; readonly complete: boolean; readonly cut: ReadCut; readonly existence?: "present" | "absent" | "partial" }
  | { readonly denied: Refusal }
  | { readonly loading: true };
/** Read every page by passing each opaque `next` back unchanged. The callback
 * receives null first. Refusals, loading, malformed/repeated cursors and a
 * capped or incomplete page fail instead of returning a partial snapshot. */
/** @throws E_PROOF_PAGE for a refused, loading, malformed, repeated, incomplete or over-limit traversal; callback errors propagate. */
// @ref LLP 3000.000.000.002#10-wave-25-day-two-friction-dx8 — proof traversal must never promote capped or partial reads to complete evidence.
export async function exhaustPages<Row>(readPage: (cursor: Cursor | null) => Promise<ProofPage<Row>>, options: ExhaustPagesOptions = {}): Promise<Row[]> {
  const maxPages = options.maxPages ?? 1_000, label = options.label ?? "paged read";
  if (!Number.isSafeInteger(maxPages) || maxPages < 1) throw new Error(`E_PROOF_PAGE: ${label}: maxPages must be a positive safe integer`);
  const rows: Row[] = [], seen = new Set<string>();
  let cursor: Cursor | null = null;
  for (let pageNumber = 1; pageNumber <= maxPages; pageNumber++) {
    const page = await readPage(cursor);
    if (!page || typeof page !== "object") throw new Error(`E_PROOF_PAGE: ${label}: page ${pageNumber} returned no read envelope`);
    if ("denied" in page) throw new Error(`E_PROOF_PAGE: ${label}: page ${pageNumber} refused ${page.denied.code}: ${page.denied.message}`);
    if ("loading" in page) throw new Error(`E_PROOF_PAGE: ${label}: page ${pageNumber} is still loading`);
    if (!Array.isArray(page.data)) throw new Error(`E_PROOF_PAGE: ${label}: page ${pageNumber} data is not an array`);
    if (page.cut !== null) throw new Error(`E_PROOF_PAGE: ${label}: page ${pageNumber} is incomplete (${page.cut === undefined ? "missing" : page.cut} cut)`);
    if (page.complete !== true) throw new Error(`E_PROOF_PAGE: ${label}: page ${pageNumber} is incomplete (complete must be true)`);
    if (page.existence === "partial") throw new Error(`E_PROOF_PAGE: ${label}: page ${pageNumber} has partial existence`);
    rows.push(...page.data);
    const next = page.next ?? null;
    if (next === null) return rows;
    if (typeof next !== "string" || next.length === 0) throw new Error(`E_PROOF_PAGE: ${label}: page ${pageNumber} returned a malformed cursor`);
    if (seen.has(next)) throw new Error(`E_PROOF_PAGE: ${label}: page ${pageNumber} returned a repeated cursor`);
    seen.add(next);
    cursor = next as Cursor;
  }
  throw new Error(`E_PROOF_PAGE: ${label}: exceeded maxPages ${maxPages}`);
}
/** Activate before taking a screenshot, particularly on loaded shared hosts. */
/** @throws page closed or browser activation failed. */
export async function frontPage(page: Page): Promise<Page> { await page.bringToFront(); return page; }
export { logsDir } from "./proof-process.ts";
/** Returns normalized visible nonempty text after it differs from previous. Hidden and editable descendants do not confer freshness. */
/** @throws observation deadline with scoped locator, last text and visible headings; selector ambiguity is propagated. */
export async function waitForFresh(locator: Locator, previous: string | null, options: number | { within?: Locator; timeoutMs?: number } = 30_000): Promise<string> {
  const timeoutMs = deadline(typeof options === "number" ? options : options.timeoutMs, 30_000);
  let substring = substringLocators.get(locator);
  locator = registeredLocator(locator);
  if (typeof options !== "number" && options.within) {
    if (scopedLocators.has(locator)) throw Error("E_PROOF_USAGE: within was supplied twice; scope renderedText or waitForFresh once");
    locator = registeredLocator(options.within.locator(locator));
    if (substring) substring = { locator: options.within.locator(substring.locator), scoped: true };
  }
  const end = performance.now() + timeoutMs;
  let count = 0, text: string | null = null, detail = "";
  while (performance.now() < end) {
    try {
      count = await bounded(locator.count(), Math.max(1, end - performance.now()), "locator count");
      if (count > 1) throw Error(`E_PROOF_AMBIGUOUS: strict mode violation: ${count} rendered matches; use a narrower within or exact: true: ${String(locator)}`);
      text = count ? await bounded(visibleText(locator, Math.max(1, Math.min(1000, end - performance.now()))), Math.max(1, end - performance.now()), "locator text") : null;
      if (text && text !== previous) return text;
    } catch (error) {
      detail = error instanceof Error && (error.name === "TimeoutError" || /timed out after/.test(error.message))
        ? "locator observation exceeded the remaining proof deadline" : String(error);
      // A strictness/selector error needs its original repair, not a timeout.
      if (error instanceof Error && error.name !== "TimeoutError" && !/timed out after/.test(error.message)) throw error;
    }
    await new Promise(resolve => setTimeout(resolve, Math.min(25, Math.max(1, end - performance.now()))));
  }
  const hint = count === 0 && substring?.scoped && await bounded(substring.locator.count(), 1000, "substring count").catch(() => 0)
    ? "; a substring match exists; pass a RegExp or `exact: false`" : "";
  const headings = await bounded(Promise.resolve().then(() => locator.page().locator('h1:visible, h2:visible, [role="heading"]:visible').allTextContents()), 1000, "visible headings").catch(() => []);
  const heading = headings.map(text => text.replace(/\s+/g, " ").trim()).filter(Boolean).slice(0, 3).join(" | ").slice(0, 300);
  throw Error(`no fresh signal after ${timeoutMs} ms: ${String(locator)}; last count=${count}, text=${JSON.stringify(text)}; ${count ? "present but unchanged" : "absent"}; previous=${JSON.stringify(previous)}; visible heading=${JSON.stringify(heading || "(none)")}${hint}${detail ? `; ${detail}` : ""}`);
}
/** Poll authoritative server truth after a receipt; a pending prediction is not a commit. */
/** @throws deadline naming label and last value; read or predicate errors propagate. */
export async function waitForServer<T>(read: () => Promise<T>, accepts: (value: T) => boolean, options: { timeoutMs?: number; label?: string } = {}): Promise<T> {
  const timeoutMs = deadline(options.timeoutMs, 30_000), end = performance.now() + timeoutMs;
  let last: T | undefined;
  while (performance.now() < end) {
    let timer: ReturnType<typeof setTimeout> | undefined;
    try {
      const value = await Promise.race([Promise.resolve().then(read), new Promise<never>((_, reject) => {
        timer = setTimeout(() => reject(Error(`${options.label ?? "server truth"} timed out after ${timeoutMs} ms; last=${JSON.stringify(last)}`)), Math.max(1, end - performance.now()));
      })]);
      last = value;
      if (accepts(value)) return value;
    } finally { clearTimeout(timer); }
    await new Promise(resolve => setTimeout(resolve, Math.min(25, Math.max(1, end - performance.now()))));
  }
  throw Error(`${options.label ?? "server truth"} timed out after ${timeoutMs} ms; last=${JSON.stringify(last)}`);
}
/** Wait until the page's own client has sampled an advanced server clock. Does not advance or replace that clock. */
/** @throws E_PROOF_USAGE for invalid advanced time; missing/disabled witness, signed-out client, page error or page-clock deadline. */
export async function waitForClock(page: Page, advancedNow: number, options: { timeoutMs?: number } = {}): Promise<number> {
  if (!Number.isSafeInteger(advancedNow) || advancedNow < 0) throw Error("E_PROOF_USAGE: waitForClock requires the clock advance reply's now");
  return waitForServer(() => page.evaluate(() => {
    const witness = (window as unknown as { __snapback4Witness?: { clock(): number } }).__snapback4Witness;
    if (!witness?.clock) throw Error("E_WITNESS_DISABLED: registerWebWitness and use witness=web before waitForClock");
    return witness.clock();
  }), now => now >= advancedNow, { ...options, label: "page client clock" });
}
/** @throws locator ambiguity, disabled/closed control, or Playwright deadline. */
export async function fillRN(locator: Locator, value: string | Parameters<Locator["setInputFiles"]>[0]): Promise<void> {
  const type = await locator.evaluate(element => element instanceof HTMLInputElement ? element.type : "text");
  if (type === "file") { await locator.setInputFiles(value); return; }
  if (typeof value !== "string") throw Error("E_PROOF_USAGE: file paths/payloads require a file input");
  if (["date", "datetime-local", "time", "month", "week"].includes(type)) { await locator.fill(value); return; }
  await locator.focus(); await locator.press("ControlOrMeta+A"); await locator.press("Backspace");
  await locator.pressSequentially(value);
}
/** @throws None at construction; ambiguous text is refused when observed. */
export function statusText(page: Page, text: string | RegExp = /^(?:Ready|pending|sent|failed)$/): Locator { return renderedText(page, text); }
/** @throws locator ambiguity, unavailable option or Playwright deadline. */
export async function selectByValue(locator: Locator, value: string): Promise<void> { await locator.selectOption({ value }); }

export { serveCheck, type ServeCheckOptions, type ServeCheckReport } from "./proof-serve-check.ts";

## Proof API (generated from source)

Every runtime and type export of `snapback4/proof` is listed below. Signatures, result fields and failure reasons are generated from TypeScript declarations and their @throws comments; qualification checks this table against the source. Playwright types such as Page and Locator come from playwright-core.

# Production: a persona demo becomes an account app

Bare `use identity` keeps guests; guests call `/auth/guest` before any read;
`use identity password` removes them. `/auth/config` is GET; production needs
persisted sessions and trusted first writers because signup grants no role.

```quarry
use identity password
table labels:
  slug: text 1..40
  unique slug
  cap 100
  immutable slug
  public 'labels are visible to signed-in accounts'
  insert <- viewer = 'service:ops'
  online only
mutation provisionLabel(slug: text 1..40):
  require viewer = 'service:ops' else NOT_OPERATOR
  return insert labels {slug}
```
Write invariants from the specification: name which current roles may read each
sensitive column, and which relationship must still hold. The packaged team recipe
lists member/admin audiences separately from its roster-wide read rule; copying a
wrong rule into intent cannot catch it. Use column `update` intent when creation
has a different appointer. Run `check` → `test` → `audit` before the account proof.

This is the minimal production bar. Profiles and teams below are optional application policy. Prove writes, outsiders and durable restart. Mint `token --service ops --release ./deployment` while serve runs, then call `/m/provisionLabel` with its bearer. No production seed door is needed. Add a root's service read branch when its writer probes existing rows. Every mutation still passes table rules (`guide deploy`).

A persona demo becomes a password-account app with self-written profiles,
a private staff directory and trusted membership enrollment. This policy is
explicit because directory privacy and enrollment authority are application decisions.

```tsx
import { useState } from "react";
import { createApp } from "snapback4/app";
import { webDevice } from "snapback4/web";
import { localStorageSessionStore } from "snapback4/auth";
import { AppProvider, useAuth, useMutation, sentResult } from "snapback4/react";
import { api } from "../snapback/generated/api";
const app=createApp({url:"http://127.0.0.1:4400",device:webDevice(),sessionStore:localStorageSessionStore()});
export function ProductionApp(){return <AppProvider app={app}><Gate /></AppProvider>;}
function Gate() {
  const auth=useAuth();
  if(auth.state==="restoring")return <p>Restoring…</p>;
  if(auth.state==="signed-out")return <PasswordForm />;
  return <AccountScreen />;
}
function PasswordForm(){
  const auth=useAuth(),[email,setEmail]=useState(""),[password,setPassword]=useState(""),[why,setWhy]=useState("");
  async function submit(signup:boolean){const result=await(signup?auth.signup(email,password):auth.login(email,password));if(!result.ok)setWhy(result.why.message);}
  return <form onSubmit={e=>{e.preventDefault();void submit(false);}}>
    <input aria-label="Email" type="email" required value={email} onChange={e=>setEmail(e.target.value)}/>
    <input aria-label="Password" type="password" required minLength={8} value={password} onChange={e=>setPassword(e.target.value)}/>
    <button>Sign in</button><button type="button" onClick={()=>void submit(true)}>Sign up</button>
    {why&&<p role="alert">{why}</p>}</form>;
}
function AccountScreen(){
  const auth=useAuth(),save=useMutation(api.register),[name,setName]=useState("");
  async function submit(){const displayName=name.trim();if(!displayName||displayName.length>80)return;const write=await save.run({displayName});if(sentResult(write)!==undefined)setName(current=>current.trim()===displayName?"":current);}
  return <><p>Account {auth.viewer}</p><input aria-label="Display name" value={name} onChange={e=>setName(e.target.value)}/>
    <button disabled={save.inFlight} onClick={()=>void submit()}>Save profile</button>
    {save.last?.state==="failed"&&<p role="alert">{save.last.why.message}</p>}
    <button onClick={()=>void auth.logout()}>Sign out</button></>;
}
```

## Production transition checklist

1. Preview `init --adapt --production`, then review and use `--apply`. It adds
   bare `use identity` only when no identity is declared, lists blocked tables,
   emits one service-seat provisioning mutation per fixture-only enum role (or
   one typed mutation for other blocked rosters) and shows its
   explicit read/insert seat grants. It names frontend `dev:` references without
   editing screens. Custom persona entries also point here, even without literal
   dev principals. Non-roster roots still need application policy.
   Run `check`, then `why N_NO_PRODUCTION_WRITER` in the project. It lists
   **all** fixture-only first writers and their blocked dependents. Its rewrite
   proposes a seat mutation for each root, and one per declared `role` enum
   value. Review these proposals: add `viewer = 'service:ops'` to the table's
   insert rule, authorize required probes and run `check` again. The CLI never
   silently grants a seat or changes the schema. Separate roster tables get
   separate proposals; other application role encodings need explicit policy.
2. Map every `dev:NAME` owner, fixture role and relationship to a real account
   principal. Signup grants no role. Provision the initial roster with the
   seat; `grantStaff` below is the concrete first-role example.
3. Persist sessions with `localStorageSessionStore()` in browsers and
   `secureStoreSessionStore(SecureStore)` from `snapback4-expo` on native.
   Device-less identity apps require an explicit `sessionStore` in TypeScript.
4. Keep the password form available alongside the development persona picker.
   Fetch `/auth/config`, and render personas only when `development === true`.
   `serve` reports false and refuses personas. Do not derive a role from a
   selector, query string or principal prefix: read the application's roster.
5. Use the authorized directory and shared-principal controls below, then run
   the account/outsider/restart acceptance at the end of this card. See
   the full auth-form and proof-kit recipes in `guide client`/`guide testing`.

## Password UI and auth transitions

`use identity guests` is guests-only,
while bare `use identity` allows both password accounts and guests. A guest
session is a session: when password accounts are enabled, app.auth.signup/login
can replace it with an account;
the SDK keeps pending writes and receipts under the guest principal, never
transfers them to the new account. AppProvider resets the guest screen's drafts
on that viewer change.

Replace `?as=alice`, `usePersonaApp` and fixed `dev:` owners with `createApp`
using a persisted session store (`guide client`, password React/Expo). Choose
`use identity password` when guests must be removed, and retain `use personas` only for
development tests. Production principals come from signup/login, not names.
`init --adapt --production` previews the identity and first-writer schema plan;
`--apply` writes it and leaves application screens unchanged. Map each listed
`dev:` location to the authenticated viewer or an authorized picker. Directory
privacy and who may invite remain explicit application policy.

`AppProvider` remounts children when the app or viewer changes. Restoring to
signed-out with the same null viewer preserves the sign-in inputs. A draft inside
PasswordForm resets when entering or leaving an account; store a mode choice
or unsent work above the provider if it must survive. `useSignedInViewer()` is
for a signed-in AccountScreen; `restoring` is not a signed-in viewer. The browser setup above persists sessions; on Expo replace webDevice with

    ) -> Result<Arc<Self>, RuntimeError> {
        let depth = match std::env::var("SNAPBACK4_POSTGRES_PIPELINE_DEPTH") {
            Ok(value) => value

                    RuntimeError::Input("SNAPBACK4_POSTGRES_PIPELINE_DEPTH must be 1..8".into())
                })?,
            Err(std::env::VarError::NotPresent) => 4,
            Err(_) => {

            }
        };
        Self::with_depth(config, namespace, tag, depth)
    }
    fn with_depth(
        config: &postgres::Config,
        namespace: &str,
        tag: Option<String>,
        depth: usize,
    ) -> Result<Arc<Self>, RuntimeError> {
        let mut c = tokio_postgres::Config::new();
        if let Some(v) = config.get_user() {
            c.user(v);
        }
        if let Some(v) = config.get_password() {
            c.password(v);
        }
        if let Some(v) = config.get_dbname() {
            c.dbname(v);
        }
        if let Some(v) = config.get_options() {
            c.options(v);
        }
        if let Some(v) = tag.as_deref().or(config.get_application_name()) {
            c.application_name(v);
        }
        for host in config.get_hosts() {
            match host {
                postgres::config::Host::Tcp(v) => {
                    c.host(v);
                }
                #[cfg(unix)]
                postgres::config::Host::Unix(v) => {
                    c.host_path(v);
                }
            }
        }
        for v in config.get_hostaddrs() {
            c.hostaddr(*v);
        }
        for v in config.get_ports() {
            c.port(*v);
        }
        c.ssl_mode(config.get_ssl_mode())
            .ssl_negotiation(config.get_ssl_negotiation())
            .channel_binding(config.get_channel_binding())
            .target_session_attrs(config.get_target_session_attrs())
            .load_balance_hosts(config.get_load_balance_hosts())
            .keepalives(config.get_keepalives())
            .keepalives_idle(config.get_keepalives_idle())
            .connect_timeout(
                config
                    .get_connect_timeout()
                    .copied()
                    .unwrap_or(std::time::Duration::from_secs(5)),
            );
        if let Some(v) = config.get_tcp_user_timeout() {
            c.tcp_user_timeout(*v);
        }
        if let Some(v) = config.get_keepalives_interval() {
            c.keepalives_interval(v);
        }
        if let Some(v) = config.get_keepalives_retries() {
            c.keepalives_retries(v);
        }
        let quoted = super::store::namespace(namespace)?;
        Ok(Arc::new(Self {
            config: c,
            setup: format!(

            available: Condvar::new(),
            next: AtomicUsize::new(0),
            depth,
        }))
    }
    fn slot(&self) -> Result<Arc<Slot>, RuntimeError> {
        self.slot_with(|| {})
    }
    // @ref LLP 3000.009.001.000.002#4.1 — reserve bounded construction, then qualify without owning the global slots mutex.
    fn slot_with(&self, constructing: impl FnOnce()) -> Result<Arc<Slot>, RuntimeError> {
        let mut slots = self.slots.lock().unwrap_or_else(|e| e.into_inner());
        loop {
            // Reservations count toward the physical cap, including failures.
            slots.ready.retain(|s| !s.client.is_closed());
            if slots.ready.len() + slots.opening < CONNECTIONS && slots.opening < CONSTRUCTORS {
                slots.opening += 1;
                break;
            }
            if !slots.ready.is_empty() {
                return Ok(slots.ready[self.next.fetch_add(1, Ordering::Relaxed) % slots.ready.len()].clone());
            }
            slots = self.available.wait(slots).unwrap_or_else(|p| p.into_inner());
        }
        drop(slots);
        // Return reservations and notify waiters even if construction panics.

//! Individually justified source-bound dispositions; never proof or authority.
// @ref LLP 3000.036.004#162-narrow-gates-and-individual-dispositions — bulk compiler-note review cannot clear security gates.
use super::*;
use serde::{Serialize,Deserialize};
use sha2::{Digest,Sha256};
const ANALYZER:&str="sec9-3";
mod binding;
mod decisions;
#[derive(Serialize,Deserialize)]
#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
struct File{version:u32,entries:BTreeMap<String,Entry>}
#[derive(Serialize,Deserialize)]
#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
struct Entry{sources:String,analyzer:String,reason:String}
#[derive(Clone,Serialize)]
pub(super) struct Disposition{pub family:Option<String>,pub id:String,pub code:String,pub site:String,pub gated:bool,pub acknowledged:bool,pub reason:Option<String>,pub scope:String}
pub(super) fn gated(code:&str)->bool{snapback4_lang::security::gated_note(code)}
fn hash(bytes:&[u8])->String{format!("{:x}",Sha256::digest(bytes))}
fn digest(s:&str)->bool{s.len()==64&&s.bytes().all(|b|b.is_ascii_hexdigit())}
pub(super) fn review(project:&Project,sources:&[(String,String)],notes:&[snapback4_lang::Diagnostic],ack:Option<(&str,&str)>)->Result<Vec<Disposition>,String>{
    review_inner(project,sources,notes,ack,false)
}
/// Read-only decisions for a caller-validated immutable saved-source selection.
pub(super) fn review_snapshot(project:&Project,sources:&[(String,String)],notes:&[snapback4_lang::Diagnostic])->Result<Vec<Disposition>,String>{
    review_inner(project,sources,notes,None,true)
}
fn review_inner(project:&Project,sources:&[(String,String)],notes:&[snapback4_lang::Diagnostic],ack:Option<(&str,&str)>,snapshot:bool)->Result<Vec<Disposition>,String>{
    if !snapshot&&project.sources().map_err(|e|e.to_string())?!=sources{return Err("Quarry sources changed during security review; rerun check".into());}
    let identities=binding::identities(notes,sources);
    let path=project.schema_dir().join("security-reviews.json");
    // Keep the lock inode in ignored generated output. Do not unlink a lock
    // while another process can be waiting on that inode. Kernel ownership
    // releases it on exit, including an interrupted writer.
    let _writer_lock=if ack.is_some(){
        let directory=project.schema_dir().join("generated");
        std::fs::create_dir_all(&directory).map_err(|e|format!("cannot create security review lock directory: {e}"))?;
        let lock=std::fs::OpenOptions::new().create(true).truncate(false).read(true).write(true).open(directory.join("security-reviews.lock")).map_err(|e|format!("cannot open security review lock: {e}"))?;
        lock.lock().map_err(|e|format!("cannot acquire security review lock: {e}"))?;Some(lock)
    }else{None};
    if !snapshot&&project.sources().map_err(|e|e.to_string())?!=sources{return Err("Quarry sources changed while waiting for security review; rerun check".into());}
    let mut file:File=match std::fs::read(&path){
        Ok(bytes)=>serde_json::from_slice(&bytes).map_err(|_|"invalid snapback/security-reviews.json; expected version 1 with per-note source/analyzer/reason records".to_string())?,
        Err(e) if e.kind()==std::io::ErrorKind::NotFound=>File{version:1,entries:BTreeMap::new()},
        Err(e)=>return Err(format!("cannot read security dispositions: {e}")),
    };
    if file.version!=1||file.entries.iter().any(|(key,e)|!digest(key)||!digest(&e.sources)||e.reason.trim().is_empty()||e.analyzer.is_empty()){return Err("invalid security disposition version/digest/reason".into());}
    if let Some((key,reason))=ack{
        if reason.trim().is_empty(){return Err("--reason needs the specification rationale for this individual note".into());}
        let selected=if key.starts_with("audience:") {
            let Some(family)=decisions::family(notes,&identities) else {return Err("no current advisory audience family; run check --json".into());};
            if family!=key{return Err("stale or unknown audience family; rerun check and review the current members".into());}
            identities.iter().zip(notes).filter(|(_,note)|decisions::audience(&note.code)).map(|(identity,_)|identity).collect::<Vec<_>>()
        } else {
            vec![identities.iter().find(|(id,_,_)|id==key).ok_or_else(||"--ack-security ID is not a current security note; run check --json".to_string())?]
        };
        for (id,fingerprint,_) in selected {
            file.entries.insert(id.clone(),Entry{sources:fingerprint.clone(),analyzer:ANALYZER.into(),reason:reason.trim().into()});
        }
        let temporary=path.with_extension(format!("json.{}.tmp",std::process::id()));
        let result=std::fs::write(&temporary,serde_json::to_vec_pretty(&file).unwrap()).and_then(|_|{
            if project.sources()?!=sources{return Err(std::io::Error::other("Quarry sources changed before disposition publication; rerun check"));}
            std::fs::rename(&temporary,&path)
        });
        if let Err(e)=result{let _=std::fs::remove_file(&temporary);return Err(format!("cannot record security disposition: {e}"));}
    }
    let family=decisions::family(notes,&identities);
    Ok(notes.iter().zip(identities).map(|(n,(key,fingerprint,scope))|{let entry=file.entries.get(&key).filter(|e|e.sources==fingerprint&&e.analyzer==ANALYZER);
        Disposition{family:decisions::audience(&n.code).then(||family.clone()).flatten(),id:key,code:n.code.clone(),site:n.site.to_string(),gated:gated(&n.code),acknowledged:entry.is_some(),scope,reason:entry.map(|e|e.reason.clone())}
    }).collect())
}
pub(super) fn pending(dispositions:&[Disposition])->bool{dispositions.iter().any(|d|d.gated&&!d.acknowledged)}
pub(super) use decisions::{print,render,count as decision_count};

application side-table fanout job. A schema that needs such reconciliation
must declare/prove it or retain a compatible bridge; it cannot claim required

Changing only a read predicate, including narrowing it then selecting the
older predicate, must not itself make captured read-through rollback refuse.
The reference identity, parent lifetime and authority provenance remain
bound. Reusing another parent, resurrecting an expired capture, or changing
capture structure does not qualify as a predicate-only change. Preserve old
captures; do not recapture a broader grant as a side effect of rollback.
Actual target read rules still authorize every read and the preview warns
when selecting older rules restores broader confidentiality exposure.
The operator is the existing same-UID filesystem owner, already capable of
editing the deployment database directly; activation is not a lesser data
role. This amendment adds no new capability/confirmation for selecting old
rules. Predicate-only means unchanged reference/capture structure and
bound parent lifetime/provenance; only the parent read predicate differs.
Writing the down declaration explicitly authorizes its old-view synthesis;
it is not inferred from a type match. Previews list transformed reverse
cells/rows separately from restoration and display lost confidentiality
clauses. Target rules are the selected source's rules: choosing old source
can intentionally broaden access, as before this amendment. The author's
commission does not introduce a second acknowledgment flag or an audience
containment proof for an explicitly authored operator migration.

### 19.3 Exact visibility before mechanical diffs

Rollback preview leads with `N records visible under release C will be
hidden from release T users`. Count only before-active authoritative rows
that become retained dormant in a table T has; show each table and bounded
ids in JSON. Separately count surviving target-visible cells that become
null/default or newly lack a retained legacy cell, and rows in tables T
does not have. The empty-cell category separates newly empty/defaulted cells
from restored empty legacy cells; the latter are not claimed as lost text.
Legacy stores cannot distinguish intentionally null old cells from nulls
materialized by an earlier rollback. Report the exact view without inventing
that provenance. This category counts cells, not distinct records across columns.
Actual expiry/deletion and rebuilt private caches are excluded from dormant
loss. Counts precede read rules and are table populations, never a promise
about a particular viewer's query results. Legacy stores lack reliable
row-birth release provenance, so the preview says `visible under`, not
`created under`. An old column newly shown as null is a factual view change;
the tool does not infer which related business text the application wanted.
Hidden-row counts and surviving-cell counts are disjoint by row presence;
newer-only rows have a table absent from T and cannot also be old-table
losses. No jobs or expiry run on dormant rows (§4). On reactivation the
original source deadline still applies: an expired lifetime can retire then,
and retention never promises that expired records revive indefinitely.

Each affected class names reverse projection or an expand/contract bridge
as the repair. Rule/guard/operation diffs follow it. Deploy preflight also
warns before future writes to new tables, widened domains or missing old
fields can make a later rollback hide rows or show empty/defaulted cells.
For these three syntactic risk classes the warning is conservative: no
false negatives for new tables, widened value domains and removed old fields;
a fitting authored projection may make a warning a false positive.
Exact reverse adoption on the post-deploy snapshot reports current effects
separately from conservative future-write risks. Previews use MAIN tables
in their private disk snapshot: read-through's connection-local temp-store
configuration cannot erase their before image. All scratch disappears on
success or refusal. Nothing changes live rows, pointers, history or time.

### 19.4 Operator inspection and retained identity

`sql --dormant --release DIR` and `sql --all-releases --release DIR` are
mutually exclusive read-only operator views under one pinned SQLite read
snapshot. The first contains retained whole/narrowed rows and retained cells;
the second includes active and retained images. Removed table names and
retained column names are queryable through generated logical application
relations; internal namespaces remain unavailable to submitted SQL. Factual
state/source metadata distinguish active rows, dormant rows and dormant
cells. A retained-cell record contains its id and retained cells, with
unprovided columns null. Conflicting images are separate labelled records,
never an arbitrary winning value. Inspection-mode reserved-name collisions
refuse instead of shadowing application columns. These modes bypass read
rules as existing owner-only SQL does; they add no HTTP/client namespace.
`all-releases` means every image still retained, not time-travel snapshots
or one invented version of every row per historical release.
`_snapback_columns` is the JSON array of actually provided application cells
on each inspection record, so a retained null is distinguishable from an
unprovided SQL NULL. This is provenance, not a missing-value substitution.

// @ref LLP 3000.011.003.000.000#16-wave-27-process-checks-dx10 — keep group identity pinned until inspection, then reap it on EOF.
import { spawn } from "node:child_process";
import { fileURLToPath } from "node:url";
process.stdin.resume();
process.on("SIGTERM", () => {}); process.on("SIGINT", () => {});
if (process.argv[2] === "--keeper") {
  process.stdin.once("end", () => process.kill(0, "SIGKILL"));
  process.stdin.once("error", () => process.kill(0, "SIGKILL"));
  process.send?.("ready");
} else {
  const guard = spawn(process.execPath, [fileURLToPath(import.meta.url), "--keeper"], {stdio:["pipe", "ignore", "inherit", "ipc"]});
  guard.stdin!.on("error", () => {});
  process.stdin.once("end", () => guard.stdin!.destroy());
  process.stdin.once("error", () => guard.stdin!.destroy());
  await new Promise<void>((resolve, reject) => { guard.once("message", () => resolve()); guard.once("error", reject); guard.once("exit", () => reject(Error("serve-check keeper exited"))); });

  child.once("exit", (code, signal) => process.send?.({type:"exited", code, signal}));

// @ref LLP 3000.011.003.000.000#1 — direct children, file logs and bounded cleanup own proof orchestration.
import { spawn, type ChildProcess } from "node:child_process";
import { closeSync, mkdirSync, openSync, readFileSync, writeFileSync } from "node:fs";
import { join, resolve } from "node:path";
import { fileURLToPath } from "node:url";
import { randomUUID } from "node:crypto";
import { setTimeout as sleep } from "node:timers/promises";
import { stripVTControlCharacters } from "node:util";

const children = new Set<ChildProcess>();
const privateGroups = new WeakMap<ChildProcess, number>();
const groupClosures = new WeakMap<ChildProcess, Promise<void>>();
process.once("exit", () => { for (const child of children) child.kill("SIGKILL"); });
export function register(child: ChildProcess): void {
  children.add(child);
  child.once("exit", () => children.delete(child));
  child.on("error", () => children.delete(child));
}
export interface ProofOptions {
  cwd?: string;
  env?: NodeJS.ProcessEnv;
  args?: readonly string[];
  timeoutMs?: number;
  stopTimeoutMs?: number;
  logsDir?: string;
  /** Keep stdout in memory only (stderr remains diagnostic evidence). */
  secret?: boolean;
  /** Internal: a frontend keeper owns this private process group. */
  detached?: boolean;
  /** Internal: the frontend observes parent death through stdin EOF. */
  livenessPipe?: boolean;
  /** Internal: a keeper-backed group owns npm and other descendant trees. */
  processGroup?: boolean;
}
export interface ProofHost {
  url: string;
  pid: number;
  logs: { stdout: string; stderr: string };
  stop(): Promise<void>;
}
export function deadline(ms: number | undefined, fallback: number): number {
  const value = ms ?? fallback;
  if (!Number.isFinite(value) || value < 1) throw new RangeError("proof timeout must be positive and finite");
  return value;
}
/** @throws deadline expires; work rejection propagates. */
export async function bounded<T>(work: Promise<T>, ms: number, label: string): Promise<T> {
  let timer: ReturnType<typeof setTimeout> | undefined;
  try {
    return await Promise.race([work, new Promise<never>((_, reject) => {
      timer = setTimeout(() => reject(Error(`${label} timed out after ${ms} ms`)), ms);
    })]);
  } finally { clearTimeout(timer); }
}
/** Attempts every cleanup, even if a preceding step fails or stalls. */
/** @throws AggregateError after every cleanup was attempted; each step has a 10-second bound. */
export async function cleanup(...steps: Array<(() => unknown | Promise<unknown>) | undefined>): Promise<void> {
  const errors: unknown[] = [];
  for (const step of steps) if (step) {
    try { await bounded(Promise.resolve().then(step), 10_000, "proof cleanup"); }
    catch (error) { errors.push(error); }
  }
  if (errors.length) throw new AggregateError(errors, "proof cleanup failed");
}
/** @throws invalid handle/deadline, host stop failure or TERM/KILL reap deadline. */
export async function stopChild(child: ChildProcess | Pick<ProofHost, "url" | "stop">, graceMs = 5000): Promise<void> {
  deadline(graceMs, 5000);
  if (child && "url" in child && typeof child.url === "string" && typeof child.stop === "function") {
    await bounded(Promise.resolve().then(() => child.stop()), graceMs + 10_000, "host stop"); return;
  }
  if (!child || !("kill" in child) || typeof child.kill !== "function" || typeof child.once !== "function"
      || !(child.exitCode === null || typeof child.exitCode === "number")
      || !(child.signalCode === null || typeof child.signalCode === "string")) {
    throw new TypeError("stopChild needs a ChildProcess or { url, stop }");
  }
  if (child.exitCode !== null || child.signalCode !== null) { await groupGone(child); return; }
  let done!: () => void;
  const exited = new Promise<void>(resolve => { done = resolve; });
  child.once("exit", done);
  child.once("error", done);
  try {
    child.kill("SIGTERM");
    try { await bounded(exited, graceMs, "SIGTERM"); }
    catch {
      child.kill("SIGKILL");
      await bounded(exited, 5000, "SIGKILL reap");
    }
  } finally { child.removeListener("exit", done); child.removeListener("error", done); }
  await groupGone(child);
}
async function groupGone(child: ChildProcess): Promise<void> {
  const pid = privateGroups.get(child);
  if (!pid || process.platform === "win32") return;
  const existing = groupClosures.get(child);
  if (existing) return existing;
  const cleanup = (async () => {
    const expires = performance.now() + 5000;
    for (;;) {
      try { process.kill(-pid, 0); }
      catch (error) {
        const code = (error as NodeJS.ErrnoException).code;
        if (code === "ESRCH") return;
        // Darwin can report EPERM while the keeper's group is retiring.
        // It still means present: wait within the same bound, never claim
        // cleanup success until the kernel reports ESRCH.
        if (code !== "EPERM") throw error;
      }
      const remaining = expires - performance.now();
      if (remaining <= 0) throw Error("proof process-group cleanup timed out after 5000 ms");
      await sleep(Math.min(10, remaining));
    }
  })();
  groupClosures.set(child, cleanup);
  return cleanup;
}
/** @throws partial, invalid or reversed PORT_LO/PORT_HI pair. */
export function portRange(env: NodeJS.ProcessEnv = process.env): string {
  const low = env.PORT_LO, high = env.PORT_HI;
  if (low === undefined && high === undefined) return "0";
  if (!low || !high || !/^\d+$/.test(low) || !/^\d+$/.test(high)
      || Number(low) < 1 || Number(high) > 65535 || Number(low) > Number(high)) {
    throw Error("PORT_LO and PORT_HI must both specify an inclusive range, 1 <= low <= high <= 65535");
  }
  return `${Number(low)}-${Number(high)}`;
}
/** App-local evidence survives harness cleanup of its temporary root. */
/** @throws None; returns the resolved explicit or cwd/logs path. */
export function logsDir(options: Pick<ProofOptions, "cwd" | "logsDir"> = {}): string {
  return resolve(options.cwd ?? ".", options.logsDir ?? "logs");
}
export function logged(command: string, args: readonly string[], options: ProofOptions = {}) {
  const cwd = resolve(options.cwd ?? "."), id = randomUUID();
  const directory = logsDir(options); mkdirSync(directory, { recursive: true, mode: 0o700 });
  const logs = { stdout: join(directory, `${id}.stdout.log`), stderr: join(directory, `${id}.stderr.log`) };
  if (options.secret) writeFileSync(logs.stdout, "stdout withheld: secret command (returned only to caller)\n", { flag: "wx", mode: 0o600 });
  const out = options.secret ? undefined : openSync(logs.stdout, "wx", 0o600), err = openSync(logs.stderr, "wx", 0o600);
  const grouped = options.processGroup === true && process.platform !== "win32";
  // Frontends are already detached group leaders with an in-group keeper.
  // Record their group too, so stop/startup cleanup cannot report success
  // before Vite plugins and other inherited descendants have left custody.
  const privateGroup = process.platform !== "win32" && (grouped || options.detached === true);
  const executable = grouped ? process.execPath : command;
  const executableArgs = grouped ? [resolve(fileURLToPath(new URL("./proof-command.js", import.meta.url))), command, ...args] : [...args];
  let child: ChildProcess;
  try { child = spawn(executable, executableArgs, { cwd, detached: grouped || (options.detached ?? false), env: { ...(options.env ?? process.env), SNAPBACK4_PROOF_LAUNCH: id }, stdio: [grouped || options.livenessPipe ? "pipe" : "ignore", options.secret ? "pipe" : out!, err, ...(grouped ? ["ipc" as const] : [])] }); }
  finally { if (out !== undefined) closeSync(out); closeSync(err); }
  if (privateGroup && child.pid) privateGroups.set(child, child.pid);
  // Secret stdout never reaches a file, even briefly. Callers still receive it.
  let stdout = "";
  child.stdout?.setEncoding("utf8");
  child.stdout?.on("data", chunk => { stdout += chunk; });
  register(child);
  child.stdin?.on("error", () => {});
  let error: Error | undefined;
  let actualPid = child.pid;
  let actualCode: number | null = null;
  let actualSignal: NodeJS.Signals | null = null;
  let startedResolve!: () => void, startedReject!: (error: Error) => void, startedSettled = false;
  const started = new Promise<void>((resolve, reject) => { startedResolve = resolve; startedReject = reject; });
  void started.catch(() => {}); // command() observes close/error instead; host startup awaits this promise.
  const didStart = () => { if (!startedSettled) { startedSettled = true; startedResolve(); } };
  const failedStart = (value: unknown) => { if (!startedSettled) { startedSettled = true; startedReject(value instanceof Error ? value : Error(String(value))); } };
  if (!grouped) didStart();
  child.on("message", message => {
    const event = message as {type?:string;pid?:number;code?:number|null;signal?:NodeJS.Signals|null;error?:string};
    if (event.type === "started" && typeof event.pid === "number") { actualPid = event.pid; didStart(); }
    if (event.type === "result") { actualCode = event.code ?? null; actualSignal = event.signal ?? null; if (event.error) error = Error(event.error); }
  });
  child.on("error", value => { error = value; failedStart(value); });
  const closed = new Promise<void>((resolve, reject) => child.once("close", () => {
    failedStart(error ?? Error("proof command exited before its process-group handshake"));
    groupGone(child).then(resolve,reject);
  }));
  // Hosts use stopChild() as their cleanup join; commands await closed directly.
  // Observe the promise here as well so an early frontend close cannot create
  // an unhandled rejection before its startup catch reaches stopChild().
  void closed.catch(() => {});
  return { child, logs, id, closed, started, pid: () => actualPid, code: () => grouped ? actualCode : child.exitCode, signal: () => grouped ? actualSignal : child.signalCode, stdout: () => options.secret ? stdout : logText(logs.stdout), error: () => error };
}
export const logText = (path: string): string => readFileSync(path, "utf8");
export async function ready(run: ReturnType<typeof logged>, parse: (line: string) => string | undefined, timeoutMs: number): Promise<string> {
  const end = performance.now() + timeoutMs;
  let outAt = 0, errAt = 0;
  while (performance.now() < end) {
    if (run.error()) throw run.error();
    if (run.child.exitCode !== null || run.child.signalCode !== null) {
      throw Error(`proof child exited before ready (${run.child.exitCode ?? run.child.signalCode}); ${run.logs.stderr}\n${logText(run.logs.stderr).slice(-4000)}`);
    }
    for (const [path, offset, stream] of [[run.logs.stdout, outAt, "out"], [run.logs.stderr, errAt, "err"]] as const) {
      const text = logText(path);
      const complete = text.lastIndexOf("\n") + 1;
      for (const line of text.slice(offset, complete).split("\n")) {
        const url = parse(stripVTControlCharacters(line).trim());
        if (url) return url;
      }
      if (stream === "out") outAt = complete; else errAt = complete;
    }
    await sleep(25);
  }
  throw Error(`proof child ready timed out after ${timeoutMs} ms; logs: ${run.logs.stdout}, ${run.logs.stderr}`);
}

// @ref LLP 3000.011.003.000.000#16-wave-27-process-checks-dx10 — npm script qualification observes failures before forced cleanup.
import { spawn, execFile } from "node:child_process";
import { promisify } from "node:util";
import { fileURLToPath } from "node:url";
import { resolve } from "node:path";
import { setTimeout as sleep } from "node:timers/promises";
import { bounded, deadline, register } from "./proof-process.ts";
import { cli } from "./proof.ts";
export interface ServeCheckOptions {
  cwd?: string;
  env?: NodeJS.ProcessEnv;
  script?: string;
  /** Deployment whose kernel lock must be released after group SIGTERM. */
  deployment: string;
  /** Startup deadline. Default 30 seconds. */
  timeoutMs?: number;
  /** Time to remain up after readiness. Default 30 seconds. */
  upMs?: number;
  stopTimeoutMs?: number;
}
export interface ServeCheckReport {
  ok: boolean;
  script: string;
  failures: Array<{code:string; message:string}>;
  ready: boolean;
  upMs: number;
  lockReleased: boolean;
}
/** Executes npm run --silent SCRIPT in a private Unix process group.
 * Credentials in script stdout are never returned or written to logs.
 * @throws invalid options, unsupported platform, or inability to inspect/reap the owned group. */
export async function serveCheck(options: ServeCheckOptions): Promise<ServeCheckReport> {
  if (process.platform === "win32") throw Error("E_SERVE_CHECK_PLATFORM: process groups require Unix");
  const script = options.script ?? "prod:serve";
  if (!/^[A-Za-z0-9_:.-]+$/.test(script) || !options.deployment) throw Error("E_PROOF_USAGE: serveCheck requires a script name and deployment");
  const startup = deadline(options.timeoutMs, 30_000), up = deadline(options.upMs, 30_000), stop = deadline(options.stopTimeoutMs, 5000);
  const cwd = resolve(options.cwd ?? "."), failures: ServeCheckReport["failures"] = [];
  const add = (code:string, message:string) => { if (!failures.some(f => f.code === code)) failures.push({code,message}); };
  const child = spawn(process.execPath, [fileURLToPath(new URL("./proof-serve-driver.js", import.meta.url)), script], {
    cwd, env:{...process.env, ...options.env}, detached:true, stdio:["pipe", "pipe", "pipe", "ipc"],
  });
  register(child); child.stdin!.on("error", () => {});
  let keeper = 0, exited = false, ready = false, buffer = "", lineCount = 0, bytes = 0;
  let spawnError: Error | undefined, cleaning = false;
  const lostDriver = () => { if (!cleaning) add("E_SERVE_DRIVER_EXIT", "serve-check driver or keeper exited before inspection completed"); };
  child.once("exit", lostDriver); child.once("disconnect", lostDriver);
  child.on("error", error => { spawnError = error; });
  child.on("message", message => { const event = message as {type:string;keeper?:number}; if (event.type === "started") keeper = event.keeper!; if (event.type === "exited") exited = true; });
  const closed = new Promise<void>(resolve => child.once("close", () => resolve()));
  child.stdout!.setEncoding("utf8"); child.stdout!.on("data", chunk => {
    bytes += Buffer.byteLength(chunk);
    if (bytes > 1024 * 1024) { add("E_SERVE_EXTRA_OUTPUT", "stdout exceeds 1 MiB; output withheld"); buffer = ""; return; }
    buffer += chunk;
    for (;;) {
      const at = buffer.indexOf("\n"); if (at < 0) break;
      const line = buffer.slice(0, at); buffer = buffer.slice(at + 1); lineCount++;
      try {
        const value = JSON.parse(line);
        if (!ready && value && typeof value === "object" && !Array.isArray(value)) ready = true;
        else add("E_SERVE_EXTRA_OUTPUT", "stdout must contain exactly one JSON object line");
      } catch { add("E_SERVE_EXTRA_OUTPUT", "non-JSON stdout (including npm banners/blank lines); use npm run --silent and keep diagnostics on stderr"); }
      if (lineCount > 1) add("E_SERVE_EXTRA_OUTPUT", "stdout must contain exactly one JSON object line");
    }
  });
  child.stderr!.resume(); // May contain credentials from application diagnostics; never persist or echo it.
  const members = async () => {
    const {stdout} = await promisify(execFile)("ps", ["-axo", "pid=,pgid=,stat="], {timeout:5000, maxBuffer:16 * 1024 * 1024});
    return stdout.trim().split("\n").filter(Boolean).map(line => {
      const match = /^\s*(\d+)\s+(\d+)\s+(\S+)\s*$/.exec(line);
      if (!match) throw Error("E_SERVE_INSPECTION: unrecognized ps row");
      return {pid:Number(match[1]), group:Number(match[2]), state:match[3]!};
    }).filter(row => row.group === child.pid && row.pid !== child.pid && row.pid !== keeper && !row.state.startsWith("Z"));
  };
  let lockReleased = false, observedUp = 0;
  try {
    const end = performance.now() + startup;
    while ((!ready || !keeper) && !exited && !spawnError && child.exitCode === null && child.signalCode === null && performance.now() < end) await sleep(20);
    if (!keeper) add("E_SERVE_START", "process-group keeper handshake was not received");
    if (spawnError) add("E_SERVE_START", "npm script could not start");
    if (!ready) add("E_SERVE_NO_JSON", "no complete JSON object line before exit/startup deadline");
    if (ready && !exited) {
      const began = performance.now();
      while (!exited && child.exitCode === null && child.signalCode === null && performance.now() - began < up) await sleep(Math.min(25, Math.max(1, up - (performance.now() - began))));
      observedUp = Math.round(performance.now() - began);
    }
    if (exited) add("E_SERVE_EARLY_EXIT", "npm serve script exited before the up interval completed");
    if (keeper && child.exitCode === null && child.signalCode === null) {
      process.kill(-child.pid!, "SIGTERM");
      const end = performance.now() + stop;
      let living = await members();
      while (living.length && performance.now() < end) { await sleep(25); living = await members(); }
      if (living.length) add("E_SERVE_CHILD_RUNNING", "child left running after group SIGTERM");
    }
    if (buffer.length) add("E_SERVE_EXTRA_OUTPUT", "stdout contains an unterminated line");
    // Observe lock BEFORE the keeper kills leftovers: forced cleanup cannot earn a pass.
    const lock = await cli(["serve", resolve(cwd, options.deployment), "--wait-stopped"], {cwd, ...(options.env ? {env:options.env} : {}), timeoutMs:7000});
    lockReleased = lock.code === 0;
    if (!lockReleased) add("E_SERVE_LOCK_HELD", "deployment lock held after group SIGTERM (or lock barrier refused)");
  } finally {
    cleaning = true;
    child.stdin!.destroy(); // keeper EOF kills only its pinned group, including itself.
    await bounded(closed, 7000, "serve-check group reap");
    child.stdout!.destroy(); child.stderr!.destroy();
  }
  // Pipe bytes may be delivered while the lock barrier is running or during final drain.
  if (buffer.length) add("E_SERVE_EXTRA_OUTPUT", "stdout contains an unterminated line");
  return {ok:failures.length === 0, script, failures, ready, upMs:observedUp, lockReleased};
}

#[test]
fn hiding_a_positive_freeze_witness_refuses_under_the_writer() {
    use crate::engine::release::{ReleaseActivation,VisibilityPolicy,HiddenNull};
    let current="table locks:\n  value: int ?\n  public 'fixture'\n  online only\ntable docs:\n  body: text <=20\n  public 'fixture'\n  immutable body when exists locks['lock']\n  online only\n";

    let (schema,programs)=compile(&target);let generation=e.generation();
    let mut activation=ReleaseActivation::new(2,Some(1));activation.visibility=Some(VisibilityPolicy{hides:vec![HiddenNull{table:"locks".into(),column:"value".into(),reason:"pending".into()}]});
    let error=e.adopt_release(schema,programs,&activation,false).unwrap_err().to_string();assert!(error.contains("lookup population"),"{error}");assert_eq!(e.generation(),generation);assert_eq!(e.store().count("locks").unwrap(),1);

const FRESH_PROBED_EXTRACTION: &str = "table roles:\n  principal: principal\n  role: enum('staff')\n  unique principal\n  read <- .principal = viewer\n  online only\ntable orders:\n  owner: principal\n  immutable owner\n  read <- .owner = viewer or exists roles[viewer, role = 'staff']\n  online only\ntable documents:\n  order: orders on delete cascade\n  names: file <=1000 ?\n  immutable order\n  read through .order\n  online only\nextract documents from orders via order: {names:names}\n";

#[test]
fn fresh_or_new_extraction_parent_uses_its_own_probe_context() {
    let mut fresh = engine(FRESH_PROBED_EXTRACTION);
    assert_eq!(fresh.store().count("orders").unwrap(), 0);
    assert_eq!(fresh.store().count("documents").unwrap(), 0);
    let old = "table existing:\n  public 'fixture'\n  online only\n";
    let mut existing = engine(old);
    adopt(&mut existing, &(old.to_owned()+FRESH_PROBED_EXTRACTION), 2).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(existing.store().count("orders").unwrap(), 0);
}

#[test]
fn retained_extraction_parent_still_refuses_changed_probe_meaning() {
    let old = FRESH_PROBED_EXTRACTION.replace("extract documents from orders via order: {names:names}\n", "");
    let mut e = engine(&old);
    let next = FRESH_PROBED_EXTRACTION.replace("read <- .principal = viewer", "read <- allow");
    let generation = e.generation();
    let error = adopt(&mut e, &next, 2).unwrap_err().to_string();
    assert!(error.contains("changed probe/index meaning"), "{error}");
    assert_eq!(e.generation(), generation);
}
